# Brigade execution log

The single source of truth for **where the work stands**. The plan says what to build; this file says what is done,
what is next, and which model tier each remaining task should run on. Update it in the same commit as the work.
**Exception (Rjae, 2026-09-09): a pull request labelled `maintenance` — the agentic workflows' own PRs,
dependency bumps included — carries no row.**

Ticket 15 · branch `master` · merges only, never rebase · commit messages `15: <Imperative summary>` ·
`make push message="15: …"` for every commit after the first.

**0.5.0 is released (2026-09-10: the session model and context columns, the schema-compatibility rule, BAP/1 additive); 0.4.1 was released 2026-09-08 (after 0.3.0 and 0.4.0 the same day), 0.2.0 on 2026-09-07 and 0.1.0 on 2026-09-06.** The full record through the release — every Status row, DONE section, plan
correction and ruling — was moved verbatim to `.context/plans/brigade-execution-log-archive.md` on 2026-09-06;
a reference of the form `see "P5-10 DONE — 0.1.0 RELEASED"` resolves there; on 2026-09-08, after 0.4.1, the twenty-one
rows that carried the 0.2.0–0.4.1 work (P5-16, P5-19, the eighteen P7 rows P7-1..P7-16 with 6b, 6c and 11b, F4 — every one `done` or `won't do`) followed, verbatim,
below the archive's marker dated 2026-09-08; on 2026-09-09 the Codex workstream's two rows (P8-1 `won't do`, F5 `done`) followed,
below the marker dated 2026-09-09. The complete post-release work list
(deferred measurements, open findings, conditional obligations, backlog) is
`.context/plans/v0.1.0-followups.md`; this file carries only the rows being actively worked.

## Start here (for a fresh session, a resumed session, or another developer)

1. Read `.context/plans/claude-code-team-messaging-implementation-plan.md` — the index — then
   `implementation/02-decisions.md` (decisions, all marked **Decided 2026-08-30** are settled),
   `implementation/08-phases.md` (phases and tasks), `implementation/13-commit-plan.md` (the commit plan).
   `.context/plans/claude-code-team-messaging-logical-plan.md` is the conceptual design behind it.
2. Read `docs/research/README.md` for what the eleven digests contain and `docs/research/decisions-2026-08-30.md`
   for the decision brief (it overrides the plan where they disagree).
3. Read `.context/plans/v0.1.0-followups.md` for everything open after 0.1.0, then find the first row below with
   status `todo`, run it at the model tier in its column, and update its row.
   On 2026-09-08 Rjae closed P5-16, P7-6c and F4 after the follow-ups triage (P7-16) under the bar that an item must be
   proven high value to be worked, and the twenty-one finished rows moved to the archive. On 2026-09-09 Rjae closed the
   Codex workstream (P8-1, `won't do`; F5 `done`; both in the archive): Codex has no supported way to wake a live
   conversation, so a Codex member could only be a mailbox, never a peer. OpenAI models are used by keeping Claude Code as
   the session and adding OpenAI's `openai/codex-plugin-cc`; Brigade needs no code for that. The brief
   (`.context/plans/codex-team-participation.md`) and its P0 evidence (`docs/experiments/codex-participation.md`) stay as
   the record. On 2026-09-10 Rjae chartered the agentic-workflows brief (`.context/plans/agentic-workflows.md`, v4) and
   rows P9-1..P9-7 were opened and done the same day, in one commit. On 2026-09-10 Rjae also ruled that
   `brigade sessions` should report the **model** and the **context**, and — revising the same day's first ruling —
   that the **transcript is the source**: a plugin cannot supply `statusLine` (only `subagentStatusLine`), so the
   statusline route would have needed every member of every team to hand-edit their own `settings.json`, and a
   roster column that is blank for whoever did not is worse than no column. **Usage limits and the context window
   are dropped** — neither exists on disk at all. Reading the transcript needed no T10 exception: T10 governs what
   goes on the wire, and it was `hook.go`'s own "never read" comment, stricter than the rule it cited, that was
   relaxed. The brief is `.context/plans/session-properties.md` (**v2**, shipped); its rows **P10-1..P10-4 are
   `done`**, landed in one commit, and **P10-5** (schema compatibility in both directions, Rjae's ruling the same
   day) followed in the next; the administrator's plugin-only migration path is charted as
   `.context/plans/backend-upgrade.md` (**P12-1..P12-3, awaiting charter**). **P11-1** (the one-team-many-repositories feasibility study,
   `.context/plans/multi-repo-team.md`, Frank's session) is **`done`** — the answer is "works as-is, docs only",
   recorded in `docs/experiments/E6-multi-repo.md`; the docs change it names landed as **P11-4** and the repository name on the roster as **P11-5** (Trello card 18). **Card 25** (a session's `DOING` line, kept current by its own model) has the brief `.context/plans/session-doing.md` (**v3.1, shipped**): P16-0..P16-7 done 2026-09-19/20, the acceptance green on a neutral prompt; the release follows. **Cards 32 and 33** (the open team file with its `sync` member; folder sync through sync adapters, Syncthing bundled) have the plan `.context/plans/folder-sync.md`: P17-1, P17-2 and P18-1..P18-4 done 2026-09-22, P18-5 (the real-binary smoke) and P18-6 (the adversarial and pre-release reviews, and their fixes) done 2026-09-23, P18-7 (release 0.11.0, the migration on both hosted projects) to do.
   After P10 the next substantial work is a
   second adapter, which Rjae will chart as its own brief; nothing is to be started on it until then.

## Model tier policy

The user's Fable 5 usage is a hard budget (64% consumed at the time of planning). Run **Fable 5** only where the
quality difference matters; run **Opus 5** wherever it can succeed. If the Fable budget runs out while
Fable-tier tasks remain, **stop and wait for the reset** rather than downgrading them — the user stated this
explicitly and prefers waiting to a subpar implementation. The user may also hand the work to another developer
with her own Fable 5 session on this machine, which is why this log lives in the repository.

- **Fable 5**: protocol/schema design, Supabase SQL (RLS, RPCs, triggers), the security path (frame, sanitiser,
  socket poster, inbound policy, watcher), the conformance suite's design, adversarial review/verification passes,
  and any task whose acceptance criteria include a negative security test.
- **Opus 5**: scaffolding, Makefile/CI/goreleaser, the bootstrap script, docs, running scripted experiments and
  recording their results, test plumbing, mechanical ports, evidence collection.

**Cadence within a Fable-tier task (decided by Rjae, 2026-08-30).** Run **one author agent plus one full adversarial
verifier** — roughly 4–6 Fable subagents per task. No multi-lens design panels, no separate completeness critic. The
evidence for putting the budget here: the adversarial pass found **7** assertions passing for the wrong reason in E0-1
and **15** in E0-2 (including a soak that would have passed having sent zero messages, and a "50 concurrent senders"
claim that was never actually measured). Both authors reported fully green beforehand and neither caught its own gaps.
The design panels, by contrast, mostly yielded nice-to-have extras. So when the budget is trimmed, the verifier is what
survives. Prompt the verifier to assume a pass is for the wrong reason, to check that each assertion *could* fail, to
demand positive controls, and to strengthen weak checks in place before reporting. When the driver session's main loop
is a smaller model, Fable-tier work goes to subagents (`model: 'fable'`), never inline.

No CI pin on which skills exist (the 2026-09-08 open-by-default ruling), no change to the CLI or its output. **Three blockers on PR #8**, all of them counting errors in the surfaces the change touched: the reworded rule 4 had dropped a sentence that was not this change's to drop ("Name a `brigade <verb>` ONLY if `/tmp/brigade-notes/brigade --help` lists that verb"), weakening the writer prompt's round-1 CLI grounding while the PR body claimed the hunk kept its check; `README.md:130`'s repository map still said "the four skills"; and `docs/security.md:263`, which enumerates the user-invocable skills shipping a Bash grant, named only `join` and `update`. The fixer agent took the second and third (`fac1ec2`, adding that the `sessions` grant is the narrowest of the three), the first was restored by hand. The branch was **stacked on PR #7** because local `master` sat one commit ahead of `origin/master` at `3928769`; left stacked, merge order noted on the PR (#7 first) rather than rebased. **A second, independent review** — asked of `brigade-53` on Rjae's machine over Brigade, which stood down from the duplicate work its session name implied (clean tree, nothing written) — returned seven findings, six of them acted on and every one verified against the tree first. The two that matter: **`CHANGELOG.md:48` still said the skill "does not exist" in the present tense**, 34 lines below the `## [Unreleased]` entry announcing it, and it is the only one of the five prose sites an end user reads and an input the release-notes gate points both its agents at (`release-notes.yml:80`, `:156`) — a false present-tense claim about the current tree is not history, so it took the same past-tense treatment as the other four; and **the skill's rules 2 and 3 were written around a stdout/stderr distinction the model cannot observe**, since the Bash tool hands it one text result, which `plugin/bin/brigade:158` makes concrete — the first use on a machine prints `brigade: first use: downloading …` to stderr and then execs, so a successful first run carries a non-roster line that rule 2 forbade printing and rule 3 did not cover. Both rules now turn on the exit status and on "what it printed", with the first-use line named. Also: the preamble asserted `$ARGUMENTS` "is empty or `--all`" as fact while rule 1 tested a third case; `--all` now matches ignoring surrounding whitespace; `docs/security.md` drops "one verb rather than the whole CLI", which was equally true of `update`'s `Bash(claude plugin:*)`, for the stronger true claim (one read-only verb, against every `brigade` verb and against installing and removing software); and the `/brigade:roster` sentinel is gone — `roster` is the name this repository's own prose uses for the roster feature (`plugin/README.md:27`, `docs/setup.md:195`, `CHANGELOG.md:14`, this row's own title), so it was the next `sessions`, and `absentSkill(t)` now derives a name the checkout does not carry instead of hard-coding one. The seventh finding, that `Bash(brigade sessions:*)` prefix-matches the `--json` form rule 1 forbids in prose, was checked and wanted no change: `sessions.go` sanitises the same record either way. Finding 5 is the one that widened the change — the prompt that motivated this row ("brigade sessions" typed as words) reaches `brigade:team-messaging`, not this skill, whose `disable-model-invocation: true` is correct, so the reported defect still reproduced for anyone asking in words; team-messaging gains one rendering rule scoped to *showing* the roster, which leaves the model free when it reads the roster to address a message. **A third round on the same review** caught two consequences of that widening, both the CHANGELOG-48 class — a prose site left standing by a change made after it — and one of them written in this PR's own first commit: `docs/setup.md:198` still said asking in words gets the roster "formatted however that turn's session saw fit", which `2a5b4a8` made false and which the new changelog bullet contradicts outright, so the user-facing document and the changelog entry disagreed inside one PR (now states that team-messaging carries the rule too, while being honest that it is one rule in a skill the session may not have in play, and that only the slash command is bound to the passthrough); and the new rule said "the roster", a word `team-messaging` already uses six lines above at `:48` to label `brigade team members`, so a model showing that command had to guess whether the rule governed it — the rule now names `brigade sessions` and `brigade team members` explicitly and covers both, being the same failure mode on the same kind of output. `absentSkill`'s first candidate is the bare `no-such-skill`, so one spelling serves both tests. A fourth round found the **fourth instance of the same class, in the same file as the first**: the `## [Unreleased]` bullet added for the team-messaging rule was itself never revisited, so it promised the steadier prose path with no caveat while `docs/setup.md` hedged it and `docs/security.md` measures the skill as **never loaded once in 98 interactive sessions** (`:126`, `:270`) — three documents, three different claims, and the changelog is the one that ships to the release page — and it described the pre-widening rule, "showing you the roster", the exact word removed from the rule itself for being `brigade team members`'s label six lines above. Rewritten with both commands named and the not-identical caveat. The review also settled that `setup.md`'s hedge **understates** rather than overstates: the house measurement is stronger than "may not have it in play", so the hedge stands unchanged and the measurement stays out of the getting-started document. **The class, named plainly since it cost four fixes here:** every instance is a *description of a rule living in a different file from the rule*, written before the rule changed. Nothing mechanical catches it — `release-notes-lint.sh` checks that names exist, not that claims are true, and the 2026-09-08 open-by-default ruling keeps CI out of prose — so the only control is grepping for every place that describes a behaviour before changing the behaviour. Four instances in one change argues for that habit, not for a new gate. **A fifth instance arrived by a different mechanism** and is worth separating from the other four: `docs/claude-code-usage.md` — the usage guide `CLAUDE.md` designates, which tells the next agent what the plugin ships — enumerates the plugin's skills, and it **did not exist when this change was written**; it landed on master in `6f16342`/`678ed47` two days later, already naming four. So grepping before changing the behaviour could not have caught it: the description was written after the rule. The control for this one is re-grepping the enumeration surfaces **after every merge of master**, not only before the change — a long-lived branch acquires descriptions of itself that it never wrote. Found by the repository reviewer on `293e178` and confirmed by an independent sweep of every file naming two or more `/brigade:` skills; `docs/experiments/E5-release.md:956` (0.4.0's "two skills") and the log archive are historical and correctly untouched. **A sixth instance, by a third mechanism: a stale *pronoun*, not a stale description.** The `docs/security.md` §5 paragraph this change edited ends on the house measurement — 98 interactive sessions that saw a hostile message, "**the skill was never loaded once**", "Do not plan around it" — whose subject was `brigade:team-messaging` when it was written (§3, `:125`). Inserting two sentences about the `sessions` grant in front of it moved the nearest antecedent of both "Declaring it" and "the skill" onto the new skill, where the claim is false by construction (a `disable-model-invocation: true`, user-invoked-only skill that did not exist when those sessions ran) and the advice would read as *do not rely on the deterministic path this change ships* — in the one document where an ambiguous subject costs a wrong conclusion about the posture. Grepping the descriptions of a behaviour cannot catch this: nothing described anything wrongly, an insertion re-pointed a pronoun. The control is that **a pronoun near an insertion point is an edit to the pronoun**: name the subject whenever text lands between it and its referent. Fixed by naming both subjects (`Declaring allowed-tools`, `brigade:team-messaging was never loaded once`) and splitting the paragraph so the measurement stays contiguous with the skill it measures, with the three user-invocable grants in their own paragraph saying outright that the §3 measurement says nothing about a skill the model cannot load. Editing `docs/security.md` is otherwise off-limits to the fixer agent (`developer.md`); the review asked for it by file and line and gave the reason, which is the ask that rule wants **Landed and released (Rjae's word, 2026-09-13).** Master moved under the branch three more times during the day (`7f4b3ae`, `771f5de`, the 0.6.0 release); each merged in, the last by `brigade-53` from an isolated worktree once Rjae asked it to land the PR (`cc29596`: master's P11-5 text kept, this row beneath it). The reviewer's run on that head posted one real finding that three prior review rounds had missed — inserting the two `sessions` sentences in `docs/security.md` made "the skill was never loaded once" read as a claim about `sessions` — and `claude.yml`'s fixer pushed `3f333fb` (the measurement re-attached to `brigade:team-messaging` by name, the three grants in their own paragraph, no claim changed). **Three mechanics of the loop, measured on this PR:** (1) a `claude[bot]` push holds its workflows at `action_required` (`fac1ec2` on the 11th, `3f333fb` today), so the loop cannot close a review round without a human approving the ci run — approved ci only, never the reviewer's; (2) the reviewer's `--max-turns 30` (`review-pull-request.yml:126`, against 50 in `release-notes.yml` and 60 in `claude.yml`) died three times on this branch (988a92b no verdict; 293e178 and cc29596 a verdict, then a red job at 31–32 turns), each stale `CHANGES_REQUESTED` dismissed with the fixing commit named; (3) GitHub's `mergeStateStatus` lagged the re-run of a red `fast` and refused a direct squash with every required check green, so `gh pr merge --squash --auto` was armed and GitHub merged on its own recompute. That red `fast` (run 34778172498 attempt 1, job 103780045371, shuffle seed 1789328254153052106) is a survivor of `103ad8d`'s sweep: `TestReleaseSurvivesAWatcherRestart` passed every assertion and failed in `t.TempDir()`'s RemoveAll with `brigade-state: directory not empty` — a writer still in the state directory after `watch.Run` had returned 0, on code byte-identical to master's; card 20's, untouched here. Merged as **`2731453`** (`19: Add /brigade:sessions, a verbatim passthrough for the roster (#8)`, retitled from `15:` under the `<card>:` rule; Trello card 19 verified from the card). **Released as 0.6.1 the same hour:** `b340791` names the section, `37941b8` `19: Release 0.6.1`, tag `v0.6.1` published 20:09:36Z with the four binaries and `checksums.txt`, goreleaser byte-identical to `make cross`, `make test` 45/0/1 on both pushes; on `37941b8` all four of master's jobs green — `fast` (checksums-check), `macos`, `reproducibility`, `supabase`. **The notes gate passed in round 1** (run 34779894742): `ok: /brigade:sessions exists` read from the tag's `plugin/skills/`, lint clean, reviewer APPROVED, and the published notes lead with the skill — the v0.5.0 slip's name, now real, is the first thing the gate proved from the tree. **One defect in the release path, found here:** `release.yml`'s single `gh workflow run` produced **two** `workflow_dispatch` runs in the same second (34779894653 and 34779894742, both `github-actions[bot]`); both ran the whole gate, both published the same notes (idempotent), and both opened the announcement issue, because the step's `gh issue list --search` guard cannot see an issue created one second earlier (#10 and #11, byte-identical; #11 closed as the duplicate). A concurrency group keyed on the tag, or a list-based guard, would close it; not this row's to fix |

| P11-8 | **Install and update, settled against Anthropic's guidance and re-measured** (Trello card 23, Rjae, 2026-09-14) — after a day of circles Rjae restated the goal: simple to install, simple to upgrade, for non-technical users; security is the harness's job. A `claude-code-guide` agent fetched the documentation: project scope *is* the committed `enabledPlugins` entry ("install for all collaborators on this repository, which adds the plugin to `.claude/settings.json`"); settings precedence is project over user; a committed marketplace is added on trust without a prompt; marketplaces can `autoUpdate` (background, interactive, then a `/reload-plugins` prompt; off by default for third-party marketplaces). Longest job 5m41s (run 34778172505); all fifteen inside six minutes. **Chosen:** `--max-turns 70`, by the rule "at least 1.5 × the largest observed turn count, rounded up to the next ten" (1.5 × 42 = 63; neither 50 nor 60 clears it, so this budget matches neither of the other two workflows, and the file says why; re-measure when a session reaches 47); `timeout-minutes: 30` kept by ci.yml's rule (at least 2 × the longest observed job, 11m22s — 30 also covers a full 70-turn session at the slowest observed ~8.5 s/turn twice over). **`continue-on-error: true` on the action step**, the `d684bca` shape: "Verify a review was submitted" is now the job's verdict — it runs after a continued failure without `if: always()` (the job's status stays green, so the default `success()` holds), a claude review at `HEAD_SHA` passes and none fails; the step gains `id: review` and the verify step's error line carries `steps.review.outcome`, so a red job says whether the agent step failed or ran clean and never submitted. | P14-2 | **The fixer's pushes no longer wait for a human: the fork-PR approval policy gates only accounts new to GitHub** (Trello card 22 "Workflow fixes", item 2; Rjae's ruling 2026-09-16: "let's make the changes so that this stuff runs and finishes autonomously") — a fixer push (the Claude App token's `synchronize`) held `ci` and `review pr` at `action_required` on `029cd34` (PR #7), `fac1ec2` and `3f333fb` (PR #8), so no review round could close without a human approving the held runs; on PR #8 only `ci` was approved, the reviewer's run 34778601684 never executed, and the head that merged was never reviewed. Why here and never in the owner's other repositories: GitHub's policy "applies to pull requests triggered by either the pull request author or the actor of the pull request event" and exists only on public repositories (the API answers `Fork PR approval is not allowed for private repositories` for `brigade-adapter-kafka`, `drbl-app` and `drbl-api`); `brigade` is the organization's one public repository, and under `first_time_contributors` the bot never qualifies because every merge is a squash commit authored by the owner, so it is a first-time contributor forever. The first-runs doc named `first_time_contributors_new_to_github` as the remaining step on 2026-09-10 and left "whether the bot is exempt" to the next fixer push, which answered it (not exempt) without the answer being recorded | done | Fable | this commit — **setting changed 2026-09-16** with `gh api -X PUT …/actions/permissions/fork-pr-contributor-approval -f approval_policy=first_time_contributors_new_to_github`, at the repository and at the organization (both read `first_time_contributors` before and `first_time_contributors_new_to_github` after; the organization value is the default for its public repositories, so both were set to leave no ambiguity). What it exposes: an outside account that is not new on GitHub may now run `ci.yml` on a pull request, forks included, without approval — fork runs receive no secrets, `deploy-staging` runs only on `master`, the reviewer refuses fork heads (`head.repo.full_name == github.repository`) and the fixer runs only on the reviewer's verdict, so the exposure is Blacksmith minutes; new accounts stay gated. Recorded in `scripts/ci/README.md` (Required GitHub configuration, a `Setting` row), `docs/experiments/agentic-workflows-first-runs.md` finding 1 (the open question answered, dated) and `docs/claude-code-usage.md` (the loop sentence now names the setting it rests on). **Measurement pending:** GitHub does not document whether a bot account counts as "new to GitHub"; the next fixer push on a same-repository PR decides — its `ci` and `review pr` runs should start without approval. If they hold again, the fallbacks are the card's other two remedies: a repository-scoped machine-user identity for the fixer (the classic PAT stays out of the agent job, `claude.yml`'s boundary) or documenting the human step; neither was applied. Reversal is the same call with `first_time_contributors`. **Measured 2026-09-17: NOT exempt.** PR #22 (card 24 part A): the fixer's push `e849247` (login `github-actions[bot]`, name `claude[bot]`) held `ci` 35233246307 and `review pr` 35233246560 at `action_required` under `first_time_contributors_new_to_github`; approved by hand. GitHub's own bot account is not "new", so the policy is not the lever at any value; it stays set (it gates nothing the loop needs). Fallback proposed to Rjae: the hub approves its own fixer's held runs from a PAT-holding job with no agent (the `open-pr` boundary: the PAT never enters the agent job), polling the pushed head's runs for `action_required` and calling the approve endpoint — the same call a human makes; the alternative is a machine-user identity for the fixer. `docs/claude-code-usage.md` and `scripts/ci/README.md` now say plainly that a fixer push waits for a human until that lands (the card's remedy (c), in the meantime). **Closed 2026-09-19 (Rjae: yes to the fallback):** `claude.yml` gains `approve-fixer-runs`, a third job on the PAT side of the boundary — `needs: agent`, runs on every PR-bound event, `permissions: {}`, no agent — that reads the PR's current head after the agent job ends, polls `actions/runs?head_sha=` for up to two minutes, approves every run at `action_required` with the call a human made (`POST …/actions/runs/<id>/approve`, GH_ACTIONS_TOKEN), and stops after one quiet poll once runs exist; a no-op on a human push, a fix-cap hold or an agent that pushed nothing. Seven hand approvals on card 24 (PRs #22–#24) were the measurement. `docs/claude-code-usage.md` and `scripts/ci/README.md` say the loop has no human step again. Measured by the next fixer push: its `ci` and `review pr` runs must go from `action_required` to queued without a hand. |
| P14-3 | **The release and notes workflows survive a duplicated tag push; a published release is never deleted by its own cleanup** (Trello card 22 "Workflow fixes", item 3; 2026-09-16) — the card wrote "release.yml can dispatch the notes gate twice, and the announcement guard cannot survive it": one `gh workflow run release-notes.yml` producing two `workflow_dispatch` runs in the same second (34779894653 and 34779894742), both publishing the notes and both opening the announcement issue because `gh issue list --search` cannot see an issue created a second earlier (#10, #11). The run records, re-read 2026-09-16 with read-only `gh`, say otherwise: the single `git push origin v0.6.1` of `scripts/release-prep.sh:163` (one push; no `push.followTags`) started TWO `release.yml` runs, 34779853757 (created 2026-09-13T20:08:49Z) and 34779854172 (20:08:50Z), both `push`, same head 37941b8, actor Rjae, different check suites (94191345353, 94191346251), while the branch push of the same commit started one `ci` run and every other tag in the repository's history (16 release runs on 15 tags) started one. Each run ran every step (the jobs API's step timings): each goreleaser created its own draft — 388019994 and 388019992, five assets each — each ran `gh release edit v0.6.1 --draft=false --latest` at 20:09:35–36Z (the one that got there first published the draft gh's by-tag fallback found, 388019992, `published_at` 20:09:36Z, and the other's edit resolved the tag to that now-published release and re-edited it), and each ran "Dispatch the release notes" exactly once at 20:09:36Z. So the two notes runs (both created 20:09:38Z) are one dispatch per release run, not a double dispatch by one step; they ran in lockstep — "Announce, once" at 20:12:58–59Z and 20:12:59–20:13:00Z, #10 created 20:12:59Z, #11 at 20:13:00Z — one second apart, where no guard, search-backed or not, could have seen the other's issue without serialisation. The P11-6 and P11-7 rows carry the card's reading ("release.yml's single `gh workflow run` produced two `workflow_dispatch` runs"; "the double dispatch of 0.6.1 is intermittent") and are left as written; this row corrects them. The card names GitHub's duplicate delivery nowhere, but `claude.yml:103` already states "GitHub can deliver the same webhook twice" and builds its staleness guard on it; the same at-least-once assumption holds for the push that starts `release.yml`. Two further findings: draft 388019994 (name v0.6.1, five assets) was STILL an unpublished draft on the repository three days later; and, independent of duplicates, "Discard the draft" was `if: failure()` → `gh release delete "$GITHUB_REF_NAME" --yes`, which resolves the tag to the PUBLISHED release when one exists, so any failure after the Publish step (the dispatch, say) would have deleted the release just published, assets included. 0.6.2, 0.6.3 and 0.6.4 (2026-09-14) each had one release run and one notes run: the duplicate is intermittent | done | Fable | this commit — **`release.yml`, three changes:** (1) a top-level `concurrency` group `release-${{ github.ref }}`, `cancel-in-progress: false` — cancelling the run in flight would strand its draft and end it red (`if: failure()` does not fire on cancellation), cancelling the queued one would leave a red run on a tag that released fine; (2) a step "Is this tag already published?" (`id: published`) right after checkout: `gh api repos/$R/releases/tags/$TAG --jq .draft` — the tag endpoint answers for published releases only (a draft is 404 by tag), so 200 with `draft == false` writes `exists=true`, prints that the run is a duplicate delivery and does nothing else, and 404 or any API error writes `exists=false`; not `gh release view <tag>`, which falls back to a draft of that name when no published release matches. Every later step (setup-go, Guard, goreleaser, Verify, Publish, Dispatch; `success() &&` kept where it was) is gated on `steps.published.outputs.exists != 'true'`, so the second delivery ends green with a checkout and one GET. Why the duplicate must stop BEFORE goreleaser and not merely be serialised: goreleaser v2.18.0's `findRelease` uses GetReleaseByTag (published only), `createOrUpdateRelease` keeps the published state, and with `replace_existing_artifacts` unset (`.goreleaser.yaml` sets `mode: keep-existing` and nothing else) every asset upload gets HTTP 422 `already_exists` and returns Unrecoverable — a queued duplicate that reached goreleaser would fail red; (3) "Discard the draft" is now `if: failure() && steps.published.outputs.exists != 'true'` and deletes drafts by id: `gh api "repos/$R/releases?per_page=100"` with a jq filter that selects `.draft == true and .tag_name == env.TAG` and emits each `.id`, read by a loop that runs `gh api -X DELETE repos/$R/releases/<id>` on it, the listing and each delete tolerant of failure (cleanup never masks the failure that brought the step there) — never a release by tag. Header rewritten: the stale "only run … D1 rehearsal" sentence replaced by the sixteen-runs record and a paragraph on the duplicate delivery; every other comment kept. **`release-notes.yml`, two changes:** (4) `concurrency` group `release-notes-${{ inputs.tag }}`, `cancel-in-progress: false` (the `inputs` context is valid in `concurrency`; actionlint accepts it) — queued because the gate's writes are idempotent and a cancelled run would strand nothing but leave a red run; (5) "Announce, once" lists instead of searching: `gh api "repos/$R/issues?state=all&creator=github-actions%5Bbot%5D&per_page=100"` with a jq filter that drops every entry carrying a `.pull_request` (the issues endpoint lists pull requests too) and counts those whose `.title` equals `env.TITLE`, the title through `env`, newest first so the tag's own issue is on the first page; an API error now fails the step loud (`set -e` on the assignment) instead of falling through to a second issue as the old pipeline into `grep -q` did. The header's "a re-dispatch rewrites the same notes and opens no second issue" and the banner's "idempotent" are corrected to "idempotent under serialisation", with v0.6.1's two simultaneous runs stated, not deleted. **Considered and not taken:** skipping the whole gate when the issue exists — the header documents a re-dispatch that rewrites the same notes as a legitimate path and P9-8's fix cycle relies on it, so an existing issue skips the announcement only; `cancel-in-progress: true` (above); distinguishing 404 from other API errors in the published check (either way the run proceeds as a first run, and if it was in fact a duplicate goreleaser's 422 fails it red with the published release untouched, the discard step being drafts-only). **Rehearsed read-only against the live repository before the commit**, the steps' shell verbatim with a scratch `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`: the published check for `v0.6.4` → 200, draft false, `exists=true`, the duplicate-delivery line printed; for `v0.0.0-nonexistent` → `gh: Not Found (HTTP 404)`, `exists=false`, step exit 0; for `v0.6.1` → 388019992, `exists=true`. The draft listing for `TAG=v0.6.1` → exactly `388019994`; for `TAG=v0.6.4` → nothing (the loop body echoed instead of deleting). The announcement listing for "0.6.1 is out — run /brigade:update" → 2 (#10 open, #11 closed by Rjae at 20:14:36Z), for "9.9.9 is out — run /brigade:update" → 0; the bot's issues are #6 and #9–#14, no pull request among them. **The one approved write, 2026-09-16:** before, `388019994,true,"v0.6.1","v0.6.1",5` and `388019992,false,"v0.6.1","v0.6.1",5`; `gh api -X DELETE repos/appshapes/brigade/releases/388019994` exit 0; after, 388019994 → 404, 388019992 `draft false` with its five assets (`brigade_0.6.1_{darwin,linux}_{amd64,arm64}`, `checksums.txt`), `gh release view v0.6.1` → `isDraft false`, the only release tagged v0.6.1; 13 releases and 0 drafts on the repository. | P14-5 | **The developer's turn budget, measured and re-pinned; a branch pushed before the ceiling still becomes a PR, and a dead agent no longer closes its issue** (Trello card 22 "Workflow fixes", found landing item 4 through the loop; 2026-09-16) — issue #15's first run, 35153548072, hit `--max-turns 60` (`error_max_turns`, 61 turns, 20m45s) after it had reproduced the failure (6/200 under `-count=200 -race -shuffle=on`), found the writer (`go w.reopen(s)`, `attempt.go:250`, unjoined), written the fix and proved its witness, but before the gates and the push: nothing was pushed, `open-pr` was skipped (`needs: agent` had failed) and the issue sat with a dead tracking comment. The same defect as item 1 on the other agent: the budget was provisional (§7.3, "re-pin from the first ten real runs", P9-3) and the first developer run ever, 34484257940, had already used 59 of 60 | done | Fable | this commit — **measured over the seven agent runs of `claude.yml`** (`gh run list`, the logs' `num_turns`/`subtype`, `gh run view --json jobs`): fixer runs 13, 13, 15, 20, 30 turns in 100–525 s; developer runs 59 (440 s) and 61 (1245 s, the ceiling) — both developer samples AT the ceiling, so the measurement is censored. **Chosen:** `--max-turns 120` (twice the ceiling; re-measure when a session passes 80) and `timeout-minutes: 90` (twice the longest projected job: 120 turns at the observed ~20 s/turn is ~40 min plus setup). **`continue-on-error: true` on the action step**, the P14-1 shape, with the verdict moved downstream: (a) the job publishes `agent_outcome` (`steps.claude.outcome`) beside `branch_name`; (b) `open-pr`, which `needs: agent` used to skip on the failure, now runs, and when the action published no branch name it looks for a pushed `claude/issue-<n>-*` branch before concluding nothing changed, so a run that pushed and then died still gets its PR; (c) the close-when-nothing-changed step now requires `agent_outcome == success`, and a new step leaves the issue open with a comment and a red job when the agent died without a push — the old shape would have CLOSED it as "nothing to change"; (d) "Verify the fix was pushed" keeps deciding the fixer path, and a new "Fail on an undecided agent failure" step keeps the human-`@claude` paths red on a dead agent. Validated: YAML parse (ruby), actionlint in Docker (only the known `runner-label` notice), `make typecheck pull build test`. Issue #15 was re-labelled `claude` once this landed; its row is P14-6. Not measurable until a run dies after pushing: the branch recovery and the leave-open step are rehearsed only by reading. **Re-pinned 2026-09-17 from seventeen runs:** developer 90 (card 24 part A, run 35231317403) and 124 (part B, 35237622142, green) fit; part C (the additive-protocol work order) died at the 120 ceiling twice (35229062415, 35245775186, 121 turns, nothing pushed, no checklist) — censored again; fixer runs 16–58. By P14-1's rule (1.5 × the largest observed, rounded up) `--max-turns 200`, `timeout-minutes: 150` (200 turns at ~20 s/turn ≈ 67 min, twice that); re-measure past 150 turns or 75 min. The die-after-push recovery is still unexercised: both ceiling deaths pushed nothing. |
| P14-6 | **The watcher's exit waits for every writer to the state directory** (Trello card 22 "Workflow fixes", item 4; 2026-09-16) — the one survivor of the ticket-20 intermittent-failure sweep (`103ad8d`, recorded on the P11-6 row as P13-1): `TestReleaseSurvivesAWatcherRestart` (`internal/harness/watch/hold_test.go:171`) passed every assertion and then `t.TempDir`'s cleanup failed with `brigade-state: directory not empty` — CI run 34778172498 attempt 1, job 103780045371, Linux, `-test.shuffle 1789328254153052106`, on a harness tree byte-identical to master, with both watcher runs already `exit 0; stderr ""`. A writer outlived the exit, so it was a shutdown-ordering bug in the watcher rather than a timing bound, and it could hit any of the ~48 sites that stop a watcher and let `t.TempDir` clean up. Rjae's ticket-20 rule ("either permanently fix these failures, or remove them from the suite … I would rather lose test coverage than continue along with intermittent failures") chose **fix**, at the mechanism: no sleep, no time bound, no cleanup retry, no skip | done | Opus | this PR — **reproduced** on the runner with `go test ./internal/harness/watch/ -run TestReleaseSurvivesAWatcherRestart -count=200 -race -shuffle=on`: 6/200 `directory not empty` (run 35153548072, which then hit the turn ceiling before it could push; this run re-reproduced it through the witness below, 11/30 on the unfixed code). **The writer** is `go w.reopen(s)` (`attempt.go:250`), joined by nothing: a goroutine dump at the first post-exit file creation caught it inside `adapterclient.Client.Register → Call → adapterkit.Spawn`, which opens `${stateDir}/logs/adapter-<profile>.log` — creating the directory chain — and spawns a child that writes the store. It is reachable in every run of that test: the fs adapter closes the session when the first watcher stops, so the second watcher is answered `conflict:session_closed` and re-opens, which is exactly its second half. **The fix** is `internal/harness/watch/writers.go`: every goroutine that can write under the state directory — the injector (the sink file, and the seen and pending files through the pipeline), the session's command writer (an ack or a close, each an adapter call with the same log file and child) and the re-open — starts through `goWriter`, and `run` calls `joinWriters()` before its exit line and before the deferred pidfile release. The join is unconditional: a bound would be the same bug with a larger window, so `writerJoinWarn` (15 s) only decides when the wait says so in the log. The injector's old `injectorStopWait` — 15 s and then abandoned with a warning — is gone with it, and `reopen`'s `stopping()` check is now documented as a fast path, never the ordering guarantee. **The witness** is `Deps.Writers *WriterCount` (nil in production): the fixture reads the live count on the goroutine that called `Run`, the instant it returns and before the exit code is published, and `running.assertNoLiveWriters` asserts zero at every stop site in the package — plus `TestTheExitJoinsAReopenWriter`, which forces the window with the existing `Deps.Spawn` seam (it holds the re-open's `session register` inside the spawn until the exit path has logged `watch child ended`, the last step before the join) using no sleep and no bound beyond the package's documented hang catchers. **Run against the unfixed code** (the join removed, the seam kept): the forced-window test fails 3/3 in 0.03 s (`state-directory writers live when Run returned = 1`) and the fixture witness fires 11/30 on the old failing test. **After the fix**: `-run TestReleaseSurvivesAWatcherRestart -count=200 -race -shuffle=on` and the whole package at `-count=5 -race -shuffle=on` pass, and the gates (`make typecheck tidy-check`, `make lint`, `make build test`, `make deps-check schema-check`, `make plugin-check`) are green. One `CHANGELOG.md` line under `[Unreleased]`, because the wait is observable at `brigade` exit: the pidfile is not released until the writers are done. This closes the P13-1 survivor recorded on the P11-6 row; the P11-6 and P13-1 rows are untouched. |

Read-only and best effort in the strict sense: a missing file, an unreadable one, a directory in its place, malformed JSON, a document that is not an object, an absent/null/wrong-typed `oauthAccount`, an absent, empty, whitespace or wrong-typed `emailAddress` — every one answers `""` with a **diagnostic-only** error the caller may log at debug and must never act on, and a `""` answer never carries a nil error. It streams through an `io.LimitReader` at `MaxFileBytes = 64 MiB` because a real `.claude.json` is not small (it accumulates per-project history) and a cap that fits a registry entry would silently lose the default for heavy users. The two `.claude.json` paths are the only names it opens; `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/sessions/*.key` is never read (`TestPathsNamesTheTwoFiles` asserts the path list, `TestEmailWritesNothing` that the tree is byte-identical afterwards). **The option** is `label`, added to `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`'s `userConfig` and to `internal/harness/config/options.go` beside `config_dir`: `config.ParseLabelOption` normalises `""`/`account` → `LabelAccount`, `none` → `LabelNone`, anything else → that literal through `SanitizeLabel`, with a literal that sanitises away failing to `LabelNone` rather than to the account default (the only direction that cannot widen what is shared); `config.LabelOption(environ)` reads `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_LABEL`, else `BRIGADE_LABEL` **through `Trusted`** — honoured at a terminal exactly as `BRIGADE_CONFIG_DIR` is, stripped inside a session like every inherited `BRIGADE_*` (U-27: a repository's settings `env` block must not choose what a member's label says about them); `config.LabelFor(option, account)` turns the option into the label, and never calls `account` under `none`. `ParseOptions` fills the new `Options.Label`. **Where each path reads it** (the brief asks this said out loud): inside a Claude Code session, from the start facts — plugin options never reach the Bash tool, so `sessionmap.StartFacts` gains `label_option` (`omitzero`, so a file written before it existed still reads) exactly as it carries `config_dir`, written by `hook/start.go`'s `writeStartFacts`, and read by `commands.labelOption` through the existing `inv.startFacts`; outside one, from `config.LabelOption(inv.Environ)`. an earlier version of this row said the missing-file arm pins `fs.ErrNotExist`, which was true of the first commit only — `ErrNoFile` is a bare `errors.New` and wraps nothing, exactly so the `*fs.PathError` cannot reach a log line, and this sentence replaces that one; 100% statement coverage), `options_label_test.go` (the parse table, the sanitising arms, the four resolution arms including the planted `BRIGADE_LABEL` inside a session, `LabelFor`'s table and the assertion that `none` never calls the reader), `resolve_test.go` (`TestHookStartFactsCarryTheLabelOption`, three arms, the sibling of P7-11's `config_dir` acceptance), `teamsetup_test.go` (create and join defaulting, `--label` overriding, opted out, no account file at all, and the prompt offering the default with an empty answer accepting it) and `insession_test.go` (`TestTeamJoinInSessionResolvesTheLabelFromTheStartFacts`, three arms, each with a hostile `BRIGADE_LABEL=planted-by-the-repository` in the environment, plus the flag-wins arm). `ParseOptions`'s two whole-struct golden tests gained the new field. **The PR's two review rounds, folded into this row** (the second review's blocker 2 asked for the row to be corrected in place, the P15-1 way, rather than for a new one; this row describes the merged tree, not the first commit). **`ff53f00`, round 1:** (i) `emailFrom` returned `os.Open`'s `*fs.PathError` verbatim and `defaultLabel` logs whatever it gets, so the commonest failure — no file at all, on a machine that has never signed in — put the member's home directory and their `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` in a debug line the redactor does not redact (it knows registered tokens and the secret key list, not paths); the open error now maps to two new fixed-text sentinels, `ErrNoFile` (not there) and `ErrUnreadable` (there and unopenable), so `account.go`'s claim that no diagnostic carries the path became true of every error the package answers, and `noPathIn` asserts it on each failing arm rather than leaving it to a review. | P15-3 | **Every member's empty label is filled through registration, so nobody rejoins** (Trello card 24 "Label every member from their Claude account and show it in the roster", part C; owner's design 2026-09-17) — parts A and B made a roster read as people and gave a new member a default label, but every membership that already existed still had an empty `human_label`, and only `create_team` and `join_team` ever wrote one: the fill would have cost every existing member a rejoin, which the owner ruled out. Registration is the event that happens anyway — at every session start and at every watcher replacement on a plugin version change — so the plugin update itself delivers it. It is a **consent change** for existing members and that is the whole of its risk: the consent point moves from a join to the first session start after the update, which is stated in `CHANGELOG.md` under `Changed`, in `docs/security.md` at the labels paragraph and in `docs/setup.md` where the administrator reads about migrations | done | Opus | this PR — **Protocol, additive** (v1 is frozen; an addition is announced through `capabilities`, 4.7), following `41b0c4b`'s shape: `SessionRegistration` gains `human_label` (`*string`, `omitzero`, `optionalText` against `MaxHumanLabelChars`) and the 4.7 registry gains `session.human_label`. It is on the **registration only** — not on `HeartbeatRequest` and not on the watch `heartbeat` command, where a label has no meaning: the value is the harness's *default* for its principal, offered once per registration, not a session fact that changes under it. The semantics are one rule, written into `docs/protocol-v1.md` at the 4.4.2 member table, the registration paragraph and the 4.7 row: an adapter announcing the capability adopts it as the membership's `human_label` **only when the membership has none**; an existing label is **never** overwritten, by this member or any later registration; an adapter without the capability accepts the member and ignores it. `make schema` regenerated `docs/protocol-v1.schema.json` (the `maxLength` patch list in `internal/protocol/schema/schema.go` gained the member, so a forgotten cap fails loudly rather than silently); the two examples under `internal/protocol/testdata/examples/` and their copies in the specification moved together, which `TestSpecExamplesAreTheTestdataFiles` enforces in both directions. **Conformance C-45** (`c45_human_label.go`, 47 cases) is the adoption rule, gated on the capability and on `team.roster`: a principal joined with **no** label registers with one — the register result and `team members` both show it — then registers with a **different** one and the first stays in both, then registers with none at all and nothing changes. `conformance.T` gained `JoinPrincipalLabelled(name, label)` for it (`JoinPrincipal` is now that with the old default), because every fixture principal is labelled at join and the case needs the state an old membership is actually in. **Seen failing**, as the brief asks: with `store.adoptHumanLabel`'s `m.HumanLabel != ""` guard removed so the fs adapter assigns unconditionally, the whole-run summary went 46 pass/0 fail to 45/1 and C-45 reported the second registration's label standing in the register result *and* in `team members`, on all four assertions; restored, and green again. **fs adapter**: `store.adoptHumanLabel(team, principal, offered)` loads the member file, returns early when the label is non-empty or the offer is absent or empty, and otherwise writes the member file once — so a re-registration of an already-labelled member touches no file — called from `sessionRegister` after `saveSession` and before `record`, and the capability added to `describe.go`. **Supabase**: `supabase/migrations/20260917170000_session_human_label.sql` drops and re-creates `brigade.register_session` with `p_human_label text default null` appended (drop rather than `create or replace`, for `20260910193200`'s reason: a different parameter list OVERLOADS, PostgREST answers a two-candidate named call `300 Multiple Choices`, and `functions.sql` pins the `pg_proc` row count; the ACL is restated with the full new type list and `scripts/ci/advisor-lints.sql`'s expected-signature row moved with it). The membership lookup that already reads `human_label into v_label` is unchanged; the adoption sits **after every validation** — so a registration the call is about to refuse writes nothing — and **before** the branch that builds the row, so the record this call returns already carries the adopted label. It is guarded by `coalesce(…, '') = ''` in the branch *and* in the update's predicate, so two concurrent registrations cannot both write it, and an empty string counts as unlabelled whatever wrote it. The `p_human_label` cap is checked in the RPC (`brigade:invalid_input:human_label`, errcode 22023) positioned to match the protocol's own field order, so the adapter and the RPC refuse the same registration naming the same field. The `PGRST202` fallback of `compat.go` became **per migration**, which is the second blocker of the review and the one change of behaviour beyond the feature: the flat `sessionAppendedParams` + `legacyMigration` pair is now `sessionAppendedMigrations`, the appending migrations in **apply order** with their parameters and their 4.7 capabilities, and "how far behind is this backend" is one number — the count of leading entries it has, which the marker records by naming the first migration it lacks. `rpcAppended` steps back one entry at a time (skipping entries whose parameters the call never named, so it never repeats a request), `advertisedCapabilities` withholds only the missing entries' capabilities, and `forgetLegacy` runs only on a success that carried every appended parameter. Taking the parameter into the old single set would have taken `model` and `context_used_tokens` — values **and** capabilities — dark on every already-deployed project the moment this release landed, until an administrator migrated, while `docs/setup.md` promised the opposite two paragraphs above ("what you lose until you migrate is only what the migration adds"); the promise is now true, and the paragraph says the per-migration part in as many words. **Seen failing**, the same way as C-45: with `backendLevel` collapsed to return 0 for any fresh marker (the old all-or-nothing reading), `TestBackendBehindOnlyTheLabelMigrationKeepsModelAndContext` reports the heartbeat dropping both facts on a backend that stores them and `TestDescribeWithholdsTheAppendedCapabilitiesWhileLegacy` reports `session.model` and `session.context_used_tokens` withheld from a backend that has `20260910193200`; restored, both green. `session.go` sends `p_human_label` the way `p_model` is sent, so a backend without the migration answers PGRST202 and the adapter retries without it: **the values are dropped, not the call** (the a26002a rule), and a lease is never lost to a missing migration. `supabase/tests/rpc_sessions.sql` gained seven assertions (`plan(145)`): a member joined with a null label is adopted into, in the returned record and in the membership row; a second registration with a different label changes neither; a member who *chose* a label keeps it against a registration offering another; a registration with no parameter at all writes nothing; and a 129-character label is `invalid_input:human_label`. **Harness**: `sessionmap.ByPID` gains `label_option` (`omitzero`, so a map written by an older plugin still reads, and an absent value resolves to the option's own default, `account`). The **option** rides the map, never a label — the account email is read at the point of use, exactly the rule `StartFacts` follows, so no file under the state directory ever holds a member's email, which `TestTheByPIDMapCarriesTheOptionAndNeverTheEmail` asserts against the bytes on disk. `hook/start.go` resolves it through part B's `config.LabelFor` + `internal/harness/account` and sets it on the **first** registration; `watch/reopen.go` does the same from the snapshot on **every** re-registration, including the one a plugin version change causes when it replaces the watcher — sending it again costs nothing, because adopting is the adapter's decision and it never overwrites. The email is read in the watcher too, whose from-scratch environment keeps `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `HOME` through `ChildEnv`; a failed read is one fixed-text debug line without the value and an empty label, never a failed registration. **Proven** in `internal/harness/hook/humanlabel_test.go` (the four option arms — the account default, an absent option, `none`, a literal — each asserting both the wire and the option the map carries, plus the no-account-file arm that must still register) and `internal/harness/watch/humanlabel_test.go` (the same four arms driven through the re-open path with a real fake adapter, plus `TestReopenRegistrationCarriesTheLabelEveryTime`: a session closed under the watcher twice carries the label on both registrations). **Not touched**, per the brief: `.github/`, `.claude/`, `plugin/bin/VERSION`, `plugin/bin/checksums.txt`, `go.mod`'s `go` line, `docs/allowed-deps.txt`; no new module (`deps-check` still lists five); no existing wire member changed; no migration applied to any hosted project. The live Supabase tests are CI's `supabase` job to run — `supabase/` and `internal/adapters/supabase` are both touched and the PR body says so, which also records how C-45 was seen failing (the `m.HumanLabel != ""` guard of `store.adoptHumanLabel` removed: 46/0/1 → **45 passed, 1 failed, 1 skipped**, C-45 reporting all four overwrite assertions; restored, 46/0/1). **The documentation half**, on `41b0c4b`'s precedent and caught in review rather than by any gate: `docs/protocol-v1.md`'s two suite-size sentences (the "How to read" bullet and the Appendix A preamble) moved 46 → **47** and `C-01`..`C-44` → `C-01`..`C-45`; `docs/adapter-authors.md` — the audience the capability is aimed at — gained the `session.human_label` row in the capability registry, the member in the registration example and its table, the adoption rule in full (fill an empty membership label, never overwrite one) next to C-44's "two facts, not a channel", `session.human_label` in both `brigade-adapter-fs` outputs (`describe` and the `--json` report), the multi-`cap:` note extended with C-45's `cap:session.human_label` + `cap:team.roster`, its four case counts, and the two paragraphs that still said the registration carries no label and nothing later in the protocol supplies one (§*Carry `human_label` through* and §`session register`) rewritten to the fill-only rule; `internal/adapters/fs/README.md`'s advertised-capability line; and the `C-01..C-44` range comments in `internal/conformance/{case.go,doc.go,cases/all.go}` and `cmd/brigade-conformance/main.go`. Gates green: `make typecheck tidy-check`, `make lint`, `make build test`, `make deps-check schema-check`, `make plugin-check`. |
| P15-4 | **The roster is a Markdown table, not one self-labelled line per session** (Trello card 24 "Label every member from their Claude account and show it in the roster", part D) — after P15-1..P15-3 a `brigade sessions` line carried up to eight facts, several of them re-labelled on every line (`repo=`, `inbound=`, `principal=`, `model=`, `context=`, `seen `), so a reader comparing two sessions read the same prefixes over and over instead of scanning a column. Part D is the layout alone: no new fact, no wire change, `--json` untouched | done | Opus | this PR — **`internal/harness/commands/format.go`** gains `tableRow(cells ...string)`, `tableDivider(n int)` and `seenAgoCell` (which is `seenAgo` without the word the SEEN header now carries). **`sessions.go`** computes the column set in ONE pass over the records and then renders: `SESSION`, `NAME`, `STATE`, `INBOUND` and `SEEN` always, and `LABEL`, `REPO`, `MEMBER`, `PRINCIPAL`, `MODEL`, `CONTEXT` when at least one record in the result carries the fact. The flags are **table-wide**, which is the design decision this row records: a record that lacks a fact gets a **blank cell**, never a missing column, because a table's columns are fixed across its rows — the same rule `REPO`/`MODEL`/`CONTEXT` already followed, now extended to P15-1's `LABEL`/`MEMBER` pair so the two designs compose rather than conflict. `hasPrincipal` stays `hasLabel \|\| opts.All`, so `--all` is still the form that prints every full ref. **Escaping, the review's second blocker and the only security-relevant line of the change**: `protocol.Sanitize` keeps both `\|` and `\\`, and `session_name`, `human_label` and `model` are attacker-chosen remote text, so `tableRow` escapes the **backslash first and the pipe second**. Escaping the pipe alone turns a cell's own `\\\|` into `\\\\\|` — an escaped backslash followed by a LIVE delimiter — and a teammate who names a session `x\\\|idle\\\|accept` would shift every cell to its right and forge the columns a reader uses to decide who they are messaging, which is precisely what the function promises cannot happen. **Proven** in `format_test.go`: `TestTableRowEscapesTheDelimiter` (a plain cell, a bare `\|`, an **escaped** `\\\|`, a trailing backslash, a lone backslash, an empty cell, no cells, plus a positive control counting the delimiters a renderer would actually split on — a pipe preceded by an even number of backslashes) and `TestTableDivider` (its shape and its agreement with the header's column count); end to end, `listResult()`'s carol now carries `carol@example.com \\\| idle \\\| accept` as her label and `TestSessionsHumanLayout` asserts both the exact row and that **no** row has more live delimiters than the header, with `cmd/brigade/testdata/script/sessions.txtar` pinning the same label through the real binary, byte for byte, under `cmp stdout want-sessions.txt`. The tests would all have passed with the escaping deleted before this; they would not now — on the strength of the golden and of `unescapedPipes`, and of nothing else: the **third review round** deleted a `! stdout 'accept \\\| idle'` negative this row used to name beside them, because it could not fail in either direction. carol's label reads `idle` **then** `accept`, so the string appears neither in the correct output nor in the forged one, and it passed unconditionally — a renderer-visible delimiter is "a pipe preceded by an EVEN number of backslashes", which no testscript regex can express and which is the whole reason `unescapedPipes` is a Go helper. A dud under a comment promising it caught the forgery would have misled the next person to touch `tableRow`, so it is gone rather than reworded. **The trailing notes get a blank line before them** (the review's fifth blocker): a GFM renderer ends a table only at a blank line, so `(<n> offline sessions hidden; --all shows them)` and `(truncated: …)` appended straight after the last row parsed as one more row and showed under SESSION. They are now their own paragraphs, and `TestSessionsSanitisedInBothForms`'s old "no `\\n\\n` anywhere" assertion — which existed to catch a value that broke its line — became "every blank line is immediately followed by a note", which catches the same thing without forbidding the separator. **The goldens**: `sessions.txtar`'s `want-sessions.txt`, `sessions_test.go`, `workspace_test.go`, and — the review's FIRST blocker, a deterministic `make test` failure this PR's own test plan missed — `team.txtar:83`, which still pinned `^<sid>  payments-api  active  inbound=accept  …` through the real binary and the fs adapter, and now pins the header row, the divider row and bob's row. **Docs**: `plugin/skills/team-messaging/SKILL.md` ships to every session and is where the model is told how to read a roster, so it was the review's fourth blocker for the second time on this card — its command-block comment had been updated but the paragraph under it still promised `principal=<ref>` "in full" for an unlabelled session, a shape `brigade sessions` no longer prints anywhere. That paragraph now describes the **two layouts separately**, because they are two designs now: the sessions table (its column list, the table-wide blank-cell rule, `MEMBER` and the unlabelled `LABEL`+`PRINCIPAL` fallback, and the trailing notes as notes rather than rows) and `team members`, which is still one line per member with the old `principal=<ref>  (unverified)` fallback; the receiving bullet names `MEMBER` as where the short principal is. `docs/setup.md`'s `repo=` and `model=`/`context=` sentences and `plugin/README.md`'s `share_workspace_label` row name their columns, and `docs/setup.md`'s `/brigade:sessions` paragraph no longer reads as though the command's output is not a table while telling the model not to build one of its own. **Left alone and worth a decision**: `plugin/skills/sessions/SKILL.md` has the model print the output inside a fenced block, so the table renders as literal pipes rather than as a table. That is a display choice the owner made for the passthrough (the whole point of the skill is that the output is never reformatted) and changing it is not this PR's scope; raised on the PR instead. **Not touched**: the protocol, either adapter, `supabase/`, `--json` (byte for byte the same), `.github/`, `.claude/`, `plugin/bin/VERSION`, `plugin/bin/checksums.txt`, `go.mod`'s `go` line, `docs/allowed-deps.txt`. Gates green: `make typecheck tidy-check`, `make lint`, `make build test`, `make deps-check schema-check`, `make plugin-check`. |

Padding each column to its widest cell fixes that reading without touching the Markdown one, because a renderer ignores the extra spaces | done | Sonnet | this PR — **`internal/harness/commands/format.go`**: the escaping half of the old `tableRow` moves into `escapeCell(s string) string` (unchanged behaviour, backslash escaped before pipe); a new `padTable(rows [][]string) [][]string` escapes every cell of a `[header, ...rows]` matrix in one pass, measures each column's widest cell in runes AFTER escaping, then pads every cell in that column with trailing spaces to match. `tableRow` is now only `"\| " + strings.Join(cells, " \| ") + " \|"` over cells `padTable` already escaped and padded, and `tableDivider` takes the **padded header row** instead of a column count, matching each cell's width (the three-dash floor applies only when a header cell is shorter than that). **`sessions.go`**: the per-row loop appends each row's cells to a `[][]string` (header first) instead of rendering immediately; one `padTable` call after the loop escapes and pads the whole table, and the header, divider and rows are rendered from its result. **Proven**: `format_test.go`'s old `TestTableRowEscapesTheDelimiter` splits into `TestEscapeCellEscapesTheDelimiter` (the same arms, now against `escapeCell` directly) and `TestTableRowJoinsCells` (`tableRow`'s new, smaller job); a new `TestPadTablePadsToTheWidestCell` pins escaping and padding together — a cell with `\|` widens its column exactly as far as the escaped text needs, not the raw one; `TestTableDivider` takes a header slice now, with a case proving a header cell padTable widened past three characters gets a divider at least that wide rather than clipped to the three-dash floor. Every exact-string assertion that pinned the layout — `sessions_test.go`'s `TestSessionsHumanLayout`, `TestSessionsHumanLineCarriesTheHarnessFacts` and `TestSessionsAllShowsOffline`, and `workspace_test.go`'s `TestSessionsShowsTheRepository` — is rewritten against the padded output, captured from a real run through a throwaway dump test rather than hand-computed, so the many-space runs are exact; `cmd/brigade/testdata/script/sessions.txtar`'s `want-sessions.txt` golden is repadded the same way under `cmp`, and its two `--all` regex checks and `team.txtar`'s three roster checks now use `\s*` around every delimiter instead of the exact padding width, so a future column-width change does not have to retype them (`team.txtar`'s SESSION column width depends on the fs adapter's own generated id, not a fixture, so an exact-width anchor there was always going to be fragile). `--json` is byte for byte unchanged. **Not touched**: the protocol, either adapter, `supabase/`, `.github/`, `.claude/`, `plugin/bin/VERSION`, `plugin/bin/checksums.txt`, `go.mod`'s `go` line, `docs/allowed-deps.txt`, and `plugin/skills/sessions/SKILL.md` itself — the fenced block stays, and padding is what keeps it readable while it does. | P16-3 | **The verb, the option, the mode** (Trello card 25 "Use AI to keep a session name aligned with the actual work it is doing"; brief `.context/plans/session-doing.md` §5.1, §5.2 and row P16-3; owner rulings 1 — the word is `doing` —, 4 — `doing` follows whatever Claude Code allows; the send gate does not cover it —, 12 — no verb-side rate bound, no no-op-on-unchanged, no local write — and 13 — the `docs/security.md` §2 paragraph) — after P16-1 and P16-2 a roster column and a conformance contract existed, and nothing in Brigade could write the member: the model had no verb, the user had no opt-out, and the hook recorded nothing a later reminder (P16-5) could gate on. **not** the bare `Bearer <word>` rule, which is a log rule) → the path rule (`home` when longer than one character, then `(^|\s)(~|/)[^\s/]*/[^\s/]+`). **`commands/doing.go`** on `send.go`'s skeleton: `Doing(inv, DoingOptions{Clear})`, U-05 order — `len(Args) != 0` → the fixed `DoingUsage` text; `readSentence` (a sibling of `readBody`, whose messages are test-pinned: nil stdin → `empty`, a terminal → `usage` with the heredoc hint, `io.LimitReader` at 4096+1 → `too_long`) → `Clean` with HOME and the token from `inv.Environ` through `adapterkit.Getenv` → `inSession` → `resolveSession("")` → the mode gate on `t.session.DoingMode` (absent publishes; `off` refuses a publish and honours `--clear`; `unsupported` refuses both) → ONE `t.client.Heartbeat(ctx, self, &protocol.HeartbeatRequest{SessionDescription: &text})` under its own `adapterclient.RegisterTimeout` context (8 s; `call()` would give 20 s), no retry, nothing in the background, the watcher untouched, one `slog.Bool("described", …)` debug line and never the text. Success: `published: <text as sent>` / `cleared: teammates no longer see what this session is doing`; `--json {self_session_id, session_description, published, cleared, reason?, note}` with `DoingNote` on a publish and `DoingClearedNote` on a clear. A stdin read failure is `details.reason = read_error`, a sixth value beyond `Clean`'s five, exactly as `send`'s `readBody` answers it (the CHANGELOG names it). `cli/command.go` gains the `doing [--clear]` entry after `whoami`; `cli_test.go`'s six verb lists gain it. **`config/options.go`**: `share_doing`, boolean, default true (`OptionShareDoing`, `Options.ShareDoing`, the parse block a copy of `share_workspace_label`'s); `options_test.go`'s `Defaults` and `EveryOptionSet` (set to `no`, so the row is distinguishable from the default) and a `share_doing junk` row → the usual `optionErr`. **`sessionmap/bypid.go`**: `DoingMode string \`json:"doing_mode,omitzero"\`` after `LabelOption`, documented as one of five fixed words — never a sentence, never a rule, never a settings byte — with an absent-or-valid `Validate` arm; `validByPID` left unset so the exact-member pin stays green; new `sessionmap/doing_test.go`. **`policy/doing.go`**: `ScanDoingRules(claudeConfigDir, dirs, readFile) Verdict` (`VerdictNone/Blocked/Allowed`) over `DoingRuleFiles` — the user `settings.json` plus `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` under each dir and under each dir's `teamfile.Toplevel` (the repository-toplevel finder the tree already had), deduplicated — **not** `ScanNative`'s error semantics: an empty config dir → Blocked before any read; a candidate that exists but cannot be read, is over `MaxSettingsBytes` or does not parse (json/v2 rejects a duplicated member; a `permissions` that is not an object or an array member that is not a string is a parse failure) → Blocked; only `fs.ErrNotExist` is "no rules"; ask and deny are unioned over every candidate; an entry matches when its tool part globs `Bash` (bare `Bash`, `*`, `B*`) and it has no pattern (or `Bash()`), or its pattern (`*` any text; a trailing `:*` a plain prefix) matches the literal `brigade doing <<'EOF'` or `brigade doing --clear`; else an allow that is EXACTLY one of the seven spellings → Allowed; else None. A private `glob` with `*` only, so `?`, `[` and `\` in a rule mean nothing (the matcher only ever widens what counts as blocked). **`hook/start.go`**: `resolved` gains `doingRules` and `doingMode`; `resolve()` runs the scan beside `ScanNative` with dirs = [`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` when absolute, `in.Cwd`] (`doingScanDirs`; the model writes it, nothing is read from prompts or transcript; off makes `brigade doing` refuse from the next session start — the review struck the "reminds it to keep that sentence current" and "off stops the reminders" clauses, which promised P16-5, and P16-5/P16-6 add the reminder wording when it is true); §2's "What is deliberately never sent" keeps "Brigade does not send" as the verb of its list (the review caught a first wording, "Brigade itself never reads or sends", false for three of the six: the working directory is read by `workspaceLabel`, `ScanNative` and `doingScanDirs`, and the session id and transcript path are by-pid map members) and gains one sentence — the model's sentence is not an exception, Brigade reads none of them for it, it is the model's own words, refused when it contains the home directory or a path — and the new bold run-in **"One sentence your session's model writes."** from ruling 13 (what: one line, ≤ 160 characters, written by your own model in a command visible in the transcript; | P16-4 | **It survives a re-open, is dropped at a conversation switch, and opting out retracts** (Trello card 25 "Use AI to keep a session name aligned with the actual work it is doing"; brief `.context/plans/session-doing.md` §5.3 and row P16-4; owner rulings 5 — a `doing:` line lives inside one conversation: kept across `/compact` and a watcher re-open, blank after startup, `--resume`, `/clear` and fork —, 6 — session end keeps the sentence — and 13 — the `docs/security.md` §2 paragraph gains its "blanked at `/clear`" clause, deferred by P16-3 until it was true) — after P16-3 a session could publish a line, and two things then stated a falsehood: a watcher re-open (a plugin update replacing the watcher, a lost lease; invisible to the model) re-registered with the member absent and both bundled adapters wrote that absence over the sentence, so the roster went dark under a model that believed its line stood; and `/clear`, whose continue-path heartbeat carried no member, kept yesterday's sentence in the present tense over a new conversation. The opt-out had the same gap: the continue path inherited the frozen mode, so `share_doing: false` did nothing until a new session (P16-3's own "For P16-4" note). The risk of the fix is the read-back — a value the backend holds, sent again by Brigade — publishing what the verb would refuse, or a `""` sent to an adapter that refuses the member and retried forever, or a goroutine that outlives the exit (the P14-6 bug) | done | Fable | this commit. **`adapterclient.OwnDescription(ctx, sessionID) (text string, listed bool, err error)`** after `ListSessions`: `ListSessions(ctx, true)` — offline included, because the watcher asks right after its session was closed under it — the record whose `session_id` matches, its raw `session_description` or `""` when absent or empty, and `listed` saying whether the record was in the list at all (a truncated list that lacks it is `""`, `listed == false`, nil error — the caller must tell "nothing held" from "nothing seen", because a resume that omits the member blanks whatever the backend holds; the review round's blocker: the first draft folded both into `""`); only the list call's failure is an error; the caller cleans. **The watcher**: `doing_mode` rides from the by-pid map exactly as `label_option` does — `shared.doingMode`, the `newShared` parameter, `sharedSnapshot`, `snapshot()`, the `watch.go` call site — and `refreshMap` applies the map's word on every tick (the map always wins) with one Info line `doing mode changed` carrying the word (five fixed words or none, so logged by value; the line is what the opt-out test orders on). The watch `session` gains `descriptionCapable`, set in `attempt` from the describe beside `stdinCommands` (`DescriptionCapability = "session.description"` beside `StdinCommandsCapability`). In `reopen.go`, INLINE inside `reopen()` after the `human_label` block and before the `RegisterTimeout` context — never a goroutine of its own, because the re-open is the writer the exit joins (`writers.go`) and a list that outlived it would be the P14-6 bug again: when the session is `descriptionCapable` and `doingModeCarries(snap.doingMode)` (neither `off` nor `unsupported`; an ABSENT mode carries, because the verb publishes under it too and a line the verb may publish is a line the re-open must keep), `ownDoingLine()` calls `OwnDescription` under `WatchRequestTimeout` (3 s) and cleans the value with `doing.Clean(raw, HOME from w.environ through adapterkit.Getenv, w.rc.token)` — the watcher's HOME (`ChildEnv` keeps it), the messaging token as the one exact secret, and NO cwd; a non-empty clean result → `reg.SessionDescription = &text`; every miss → the member ABSENT, never `""`, which `reopen`'s default arm would send to be refused on every heartbeat, and one fixed-text Debug line without the value: a list failure `doing line not read back; the re-open carries none` with the error, an unlisted record `doing line not carried` with `reason: not_listed` (`reasonNotListed`, the watcher's own word beside `Clean`'s), an empty or refused value `doing line not carried` with the `Clean` reason only. The nudge stamp is removed best-effort (`removeDoingNudge`: `os.Remove` of **`config.DoingNudgeStamp(stateDir, pid)`** = `${stateDir}/state/<pid>.doing-nudge`, defined now beside `RegisterRetryStamp` in `config/stamps.go` so P16-5 shares it; `fs.ErrNotExist` is the wanted state, any other error a Debug line) on every miss that can have blanked a sentence the backend held — the list failure, the unlisted record (§8's truncated list), a value `Clean` refuses (a stricter watcher after a plugin update, a third-party writer's) — per §5.3's "a carry that was due and failed"; the one miss that keeps it is a listed record holding nothing (`ReasonEmpty`): nothing was held, so nothing is lost. (The first draft removed it only on a list failure and called that "the brief's rule"; the review round found §5.3 names no such rule and §8 names the truncated list explicitly, so the rule was widened and the distinction carried by `listed`.) `w.stopping() || s.heartbeatsStopped.Load()` is re-checked after the list, before `Register`. The `session re-opened` Info line gains `slog.Bool("described", carried)`. **The hook's continue path** (`start.go`, the branch where a live watcher serves the map's session): `res.doingMode = continuedDoingMode(existing.DoingMode, res.opts, res.doingRules)` — `""` and `unsupported` are returned as they are (the capability verdict is inherited: no describe on this path, absent stays absent for P16-5's prompt-hook resolution), every other word goes through `doingModeWithin(opts, rules)`, the option-and-rules half split out of `doingMode` so the fresh and the continued resolution share their arms — so the scan `resolve()` already runs and the option now land at every SessionStart but compact rather than the next session; and `blank := blankDoingLine(in, existing, res.doingMode)`, computed in `connect` where the document and the existing map are both in view and passed to `heartbeat(ctx, res, sessionID, blankDoing bool)` as a fourth parameter: false when the mode is `""` or `unsupported`; true when `in.SessionID != existing.ClaudeSessionID` (`/clear`, an in-process `/resume`; NOT a same-id re-fire such as `/reload-plugins`), whatever the option says; else true only when the new mode is `off` and the existing map's was not (the option was turned off: retracts on a same-id re-fire too; `off` before and after retracts nothing). | P16-5 | **Tell the model to keep its doing line current, where Brigade may ask** (Trello card 25 "Use AI to keep a session name aligned with the actual work it is doing"; brief `.context/plans/session-doing.md` §5.2 — the eligibility table and the one-time prompt-hook resolution —, §5.4 in full and row P16-5; the P16-0 gate resolved to "as written" — a task-notification wake-up fires `UserPromptSubmit`, so no compact-time line, no send/sessions hint, no Stop hook; owner rulings 1 — the word is `doing` —, 3 — Brigade asks the model only where the settings it can read say the call will neither prompt nor be denied, never adds a PreToolUse auto-allow, and accepts the listed blind spots including standing text outliving a mode change —, 4 — `doing` follows whatever Claude Code allows —, 6 — session end keeps the sentence — and 10 — the roster line is shown to every session whatever its inbound policy) — after P16-1..4 a session could publish a line, a teammate could read it, a re-open carried it and `/clear` blanked it, and nothing ever told a model to write one: the card's ask (a roster that says what each session is doing) was a verb nobody ran. The risk of telling it is the one the design is built around: a line that prints where the call then raises a permission dialog (housekeeping nobody asked for, stalling an unattended session) or is denied; Built: `policy.Scan` gains `UserFile`/`UserAccept`/`RepoAccept`, `Effective` reads the permission mode (bypass without a user-file accept is `refuse` with `ParityWarning()`), `hook/prompt.go` `redecide` decides again at every prompt and replaces the watcher under a changed policy, `inbox.go`'s refusal names the cause.
