| P36-1 | **The Slack gateway hears untagged thread replies and lets a session tag a person** (Trello card 106; Rjae 2026-10-16: "If a Slack user forgets to tag @brigade in their threaded reply, the reply does not make it back to the session … Is there a way to handle this very common scenario?", then "Please create a card for this. Please also include the change/fix represented by the preformatted text below [a session had tried three forms of tagging a person in a channel post and none rendered] … work on it autonomously … all the way through updating docs, pushing, and releasing") — (1) the manifest in `scripts/slack-gateway-install.sh` subscribes to `message.channels` and `message.groups` with `channels:history` and `groups:history`; `parseEventsRequest` lets a channel message through only as a thread reply without the bot's mention (`InboundSlack.implicit`; the channel-message copy of a tagged reply is dropped as "carried by the mention event", a top-level channel message as before, a channel message before `auth.test` has answered as unknown-identity; `thread_broadcast` now counts as a person's reply), `resolveSlackInbound` answers `ignore` for an implicit reply whose thread the thread table does not know (no ephemeral answer: the bot would speak in every thread of every channel it is in), and `slack-in` resolves before `markReceived`, so nothing of a stranger's thread is recorded; (2) `renderSlackText` lets Slack's user and channel mention markup through the escaping (`passMentionMarkup`; `<!here>`, `<!channel>`, `<!everyone>`, user groups and links stay literal) and `mentionNames` + `linkMentions` turn `@handle` tokens into mentions with the ids `slack-out`'s `resolveMentions` found (a bare user id as is, a name through `findUserByName` once a tick, a bot never — so the footer's own `@brigade` is never linked; a retryable Slack error leaves the message for the next tick); (3) the footer asks for the mention only while the token lacks `channels:history`, read from Slack's `x-oauth-scopes` header (`SlackApi.scopes()`, one `auth.test` per tick that has an inbox), so a redeploy before the app is reinstalled promises nothing false; (4) the installer's second run checks the token's scopes before deploying and stops with the manifest-update-and-reinstall instruction (a warning when Slack sends no header). | P43-1 | **The release-notes email's second review is bounded** (Trello card 43; Rjae 2026-10-17: "Find the root cause and fix for the send-release-notes.yml workflow failing … A quick look makes me think that an agent rejected the content of the email. The thinktech-php workflow nearly always succeeds, so what are we doing that caused it to fail?") — Root cause, from run 38265059855's artifact (2026-10-12, scheduled, 11 releases v0.17.0–v0.27.0): the lint was clean in both rounds; the first review raised three findings (egress vs requests, the conditional `crossSessionInbound` write, the Slack Messages Tab step), the writer fixed all three, and the second review refused on a fourth it had not raised (the thread-reply clause 0.24.0 narrowed). With exactly one fix cycle and a reviewer free to find new things in the last round, any miss by the first reviewer is fatal; the first send (run 36636440249) was refused the same way, three fixed and three new. The thinktech-php workflow has no reviewer: its one agent's text is sent. And a refusal moves no mark, so each refused week makes the next email longer under a 450-word cap. Fix: the first draft is kept as `notes-round1.md`; the second reviewer `diff`s it against the second draft and judges only the first review's findings and the lines the fix changed (`.claude/agents/release-notes-email.md`, "When you review the second draft"; the `Review the fix` prompt and its `diff` permission; the `Fix the draft` prompt tells the writer to change only what the findings name), with a `NOTES` line for anything outside the bound; the brief's cap is 450 plus 30 per release beyond five, to 700. Pages: `docs/developer/release/release-notes.md`, `scripts/ci/README.md`, plan `.context/plans/release-notes-email.md` §6. The 2026-10-05 scheduled run never started (GitHub skipped it; the workflow was active). Verified by preview run 39058083527 on 7c2e6a7 (2026-10-17 18:26 UTC): 20 releases, v0.17.0 to v0.34.0, 698 words of the 700 the grown cap allows; round 1 lint clean, one finding ("installed" for "hosted", 0.19.0's Slack gateway); the fix changed one word; round 2: the reviewer diffed the drafts, judged the changed line by the sources, wrote `APPROVED`, and put what it noticed under `NOTES` ("every line my first review did not name and this fix did not change stands as my first review approved it, and I did not re-read it"); the gate passed, the HTML rendered, the preview was mailed. That draft can be sent as it is: `gh workflow run send-release-notes.yml -f audience=recipients -f draft_run=39058083527`. | done | Fable | `7c2e6a7`; the record in this row's commit |

| P49-1 | **One session can go quiet: `brigade inbound` and `/brigade:quiet`** (Trello card 126; Rjae 2026-10-22: "Review `.ignored/quiet-session-handoff.md` and if viable then create a card and begin implementing … this skill must ONLY affect the session in which it is used … a compensating (reversal) skill so that the user can return to normal"; the hand-off was written the same day by her `credit-reporting-web-v2-93` session). Verified before building: the protocol's heartbeat already carries `inbound` (4.4.4, C-42), the watcher already applies a changed map value within a liveness tick and heartbeats it at once (card 53), and the one gap was the prompt hook's re-decision, which rewrote `inbound` from the option every prompt. So the by-pid map gains `inbound_decided` (the hook's decision, the ceiling) and `inbound_request` (hold or refuse, recorded by the command), `inbound` staying the effective value every reader uses (`sessionmap.TightenInbound`, accept < hold < refuse; `Validate` refuses a map whose effective value is not the stronger of the two, so a file can neither loosen a request nor carry one of accept). SessionStart writes the decision; the continue path (/clear) carries the request and its heartbeat carries the effective policy, not the bare decision; the compact refresh touches neither; the prompt hook rewrites the decision and tightens with the request, so a mode or settings change never lifts a request, and a 0.35.0 map gains the member at its first prompt. `brigade inbound [accept|hold|refuse] [--json]` (`internal/harness/commands/inbound.go`; in-session only; the status form spawns nothing) writes the map, sends one `session heartbeat` carrying `inbound` alone when the policy moved, and prints three lines: the policy with its origin, the held count with sender names, a fixed note per policy (a decided refuse is named as the ceiling nothing here lifts; a failed heartbeat adds a roster line and is not an error). Tests: `TestTightenInbound`, `TestInboundMembersValidate`, `TestInbound*` (commands), `TestPromptKeepsTheRuntimeRequest`, `TestClearAndCompactKeepTheRuntimeRequest`, `TestRuntimeHoldThroughTheMap` (accept→hold→accept through the map with a release, in the watcher), `inbound.txtar` through the real binary. The skill `/brigade:quiet [on [--to <where>] [--refuse] | off | status]` in all four packages (`plugin/skills/quiet/SKILL.md` and the Muse, Codex and Grok wordings): `on` enforces with `brigade inbound hold` (`refuse` with `--refuse`), publishes the fixed roster sentence `Quiet working session; not taking team requests.` (+ `Message <where> instead.`), and carries the standing instruction (report a frame in one line, act on none, send nothing unasked); `off` is the reversal (`brigade inbound accept`, a fresh doing line); `status` prints `brigade inbound`. Docs: `docs/team-member/use/quiet-session.md` and the use root's TL;DR, the persona root's Use row, cross-links from the held-messages pages, the four agent pages and the in-a-team root (eight skills), the package READMEs, the Muse manifest and digest, the three CI skill lists, the generated command reference, the changelog. Decided here, not asked (Rjae was not watching): the name `quiet`, the default strength `hold` with `--refuse`, no auto-reply to senders (hand-off §5.3), the reversal as the `off` form of the same skill, and no terminal `--session` form. | done | Fable | this row's commit |

| P53-1 | **Grok sessions show their name on the roster** (Trello card 132; Frank 2026-10-23: a Grok row in `brigade sessions` was named `grok`). The display name was hardcoded. Grok keeps the title `/resume` shows in that session's `summary.json` field `generated_title`, including a manual `/rename`. The hook reads it at registration and the watcher reads it before each heartbeat, from `$GROK_HOME/sessions/<encoded launch cwd>/<id>/summary.json` (`GROK_HOME` defaults to `$HOME/.grok`; a relative value disables the read). `info.id` must match. | P54-1 | **Grok 1.0.67 sessions register again** (Trello card 131; Frank 2026-10-23: a restarted Grok session still had no start pointer, so `brigade team join` and `brigade sessions` exited 11). Reproduced on Grok Build 1.0.67: SessionStart does run, `GROK_LEADER_SOCKET` is unset, and the parent is the TUI, whose `ucomm` is `grok-1.0.67-maco` (16-byte `P_comm` of `grok-1.0.67-macos-aarch64`). The 1.0.62 allowlist (`grok`, `grok-macos-aarch`, `grok-macos-x86_6`) rejected it, `decodeGrok` failed closed, and the start pointer was never written. The check now accepts a versioned installer name cut to the kernel field when at least three letters of `macos` or `linux` remain. Linux is the same asset name at 15 bytes (`grok-1.0.67-lin`), not a native measurement. Review blocker (Fable, 2026-10-23): the first form demanded that the cut land strictly inside the platform tag, so a five-character version whose field ends exactly on the tag's last byte (`grok-1.1.0-macos`, 16 bytes) was refused and 1.1.0 would have reproduced the bug; a version short enough for the cut to reach the architecture (`grok-1.2-macos-a`) was refused too. The acceptance is now one comparison — the field must be a prefix of `grok-<version>-<platform>-<arch>` for either installed architecture — so every cut position is covered, and `TestGrokKernelCommandExactPlatformNames` walks the four versions' fields on both arches. Leader mode, unknown parents and the old names are unchanged. No release in this commit: the installed 0.37.0 binary keeps the old check until one ships. Renumbered from P52-1: master already has that row for card 130, and P53-1 for card 132 | done | Grok | this row's commit |

| P56-1 | **A self-hosted webhook gateway** (Trello card 137; Whiskey 2026-10-26: connect Grok Bot to the team as one more peer, no added team infrastructure). `cmd/brigade-webhook-gateway` (`internal/gateway/webhook`) is a long-running session (harness `gateway-webhook`) on the Supabase adapter, bundled through a multi-call `adapter supabase`: every message it receives is POSTed to `--target-url`, HMAC-SHA256-signed over `v1:<ts>:<body>` with a timestamp and a delivery id, and acked only on 2xx (backoff, then a "not delivered" notice to the sender); a signed POST to a loopback-only listener, or its `send`/`sessions` subcommands, becomes a Brigade message framed "unverified sender; it approves nothing". The identity is configuration (`BRIGADE_CONFIG_DIR`, `--team-file`/`--profile`, `--session-name`). `--member` now skips every `gateway-` harness, in the CLI and in the hosted gateways' resolver (`supabase/functions/_shared/gateway.ts`, needs an Edge Function redeploy), so a gateway run as one of a member's own sessions never takes their messages. Docs: `docs/project-owner/configure/webhook-gateway.md` (systemd user unit, run script, payloads, the refresh-token-rotation reason to run it as a dedicated member). Tested with a fake backend and end to end over the fs adapter; not run against a live Supabase stack. Review blockers (Claude, 2026-10-26): the entry point built a bare `slog.NewTextHandler`, the only logger in the tree outside `adapterkit/log`, so an adapter child's error text — a Supabase auth failure can carry a JWT or an `Authorization` header — printed in the clear to journald; it now uses the redacting logger with the gateway secret registered as an exact token, and `--log-level` goes through `adapterlog.ParseLevel` instead of `slog.Level.UnmarshalText`, which also accepted `INFO` and `DEBUG+4`. The identity resolved through `config.BrigadeConfigDir`/`BrigadeStateDir`, which drop every `BRIGADE_*` when a session marker is set, so starting the gateway from a coding session's shell silently discarded `BRIGADE_CONFIG_DIR` and brought it up on the operator's own credentials — the shared-credential hazard the page warns against, with no error; `gatewayDirs` now strips the session environment by prefix (`CLAUDE`, `MUSE_`, `CODEX_`, `GROK_`, `AI_AGENT`, keeping `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`) and resolves through `adapterkit`, pinned with `CLAUDE_PID`/`MUSE_SESSION_ID`/`CODEX_THREAD_ID`/`GROK_SESSION_ID` set. `docs/security.md` gained §14.1 for the third gateway kind (the one shared secret on both directions, the loopback listener, the unverified inbound frame, the dedicated-member recommendation), added beside the existing sentences, not through them. Also: the "itself" refusal is now tested with the gateway's own row on the roster (it was reaching the 404 branch), a give-up whose notice hits a retryable backend error takes the backoff so the webhook is not re-POSTed every poll, and the `gateway-` prefix is spelled once in Go — `gateway.HarnessPrefix`/`gateway.IsHarness` in the new `internal/gateway` (`harness.go`), outside the frozen `internal/protocol`, where it first went and where it does not belong: `harness` is free text and `protocol.Validate` says nothing about the prefix, so the constant is a shared Go spelling and not a wire rule. Second review round (Claude, 2026-10-26), three blockers: this row named `protocol.HarnessGatewayPrefix`, a symbol the move had already removed; the operator page's flag table gave `--description` as defaulting to "none" while the flag defaults to `Forwards messages to a webhook and back`, which every teammate would have seen as the gateway's doing line; and §14.1 asserted that the gateway secret lives "outside the repository" with nothing enforcing it, on a page that also tells the gateway's machine to keep the team's checkout — `checkSecretFile` now refuses a relative path, a `..` component (uncleaned, the P7-11 symlink finding) and a path inside the checkout `--team-file` names, literally or symlink-resolved, as `commands.checkSecretFileOutside` does for the join secret, pinned by `TestSecretFileMustBeOutsideTheTeamsCheckout`. Also from that round: the page gives 425 with 408 and 429 as retryable, and says what a second copy started by accident does (it adopts the one remembered session, so deliveries duplicate and the receiver's `X-Brigade-Delivery` deduplication covers it). Third review round (Claude, 2026-10-26), one blocker: `GET /v1/sessions` sanitised `session_name` and `human_label` but copied `harness` into the roster raw, and `harness` is session-reported free text that `SessionRegistration.Validate` only requires to be non-empty UTF-8 — so any member could register a harness name carrying newlines and angle brackets and have it delivered verbatim in the roster document, which `doc.go`, §14.1 and the operator page all write for a model-driven reader (bounded at 32 characters by the backend's column, unbounded over the fs adapter, which caps nothing); `handleRoster` now applies `protocol.SanitizeAttribute` as `commands.format` does for the same field, the `IsHarness` filter above it keeps reading the raw value, and `TestRosterSanitisesTheHarnessName` asserts the emitted `harness` carries none of the quote, angle-bracket or newline characters. `state` and `activity` need nothing: `activity` is oneOf-validated at registration and `state` is the backend's own enum. That round's one non-blocking note — `send --from` defaulting to `Grok Bot`, so another service's operator sees teammates' messages attributed to it unless they pass the flag — is left to the owner, as the reviewer asked: card 137's first consumer is Grok Bot, and `frameInbound` already renders an absent `--from` as "a webhook caller", so an empty default would read correctly too. | P58-1 | **The webhook gateway sends the service's own key** (Trello card 142; Whiskey 2026-10-26: a Grok Bot routine's webhook wants its sender key in an `Authorization` header, and the gateway's headers were hard-coded while a URL with credentials is refused). `--header-file <path>` names a file of `Name: value` lines (blank lines and `#` comments ignored, at most 16, one per name in any case) read with `adapterkit.ReadStrict`, so it must be a regular 0600 file owned by the gateway's user; its location follows `--secret-file`'s rule — `checkSecretFile` is now `checkPrivateFile(flag, …)`, which names the flag it checks — so it is absolute, has no `..` component and is outside the checkout `--team-file` names. `parseHeaderFile` (`internal/gateway/webhook/headers.go`) refuses `Content-Type`, `Content-Length`, `Host`, `User-Agent` and every `X-Brigade-*`, case-insensitively, and `New` refuses them on a hand-built `Config.ExtraHeaders` too; `post()` sets the extra headers first and the gateway's own after, so nothing in the file can change what is signed. Every value, and the credential after a scheme such as `Bearer`, is registered with the redactor; no refusal quotes a value or a name. Tests: `TestDeliverSendsTheExtraHeaders` (the receiver still verifies the signature), `TestExtraHeadersCannotOverrideTheGatewaysOwn`, `TestParseHeaderFile`, `TestHeaderValuesAreRedacted`, `TestRunRefusesAnUnsafeHeaderFile`. Docs: the operator page's flag table and a "Send the service's own key" section with the Grok Bot routine example. Review blockers (Claude, 2026-10-26): `headerSecrets` registered every value whole with no length floor, and a redactor token is a plain substring blanked wherever it occurs, so the test's own `x-routine-tag: brigade` would have erased `brigade` from `brigade-webhook-gateway/<version>`, from the header file's path in a "cannot read" diagnostic and from the adapter child's argv, while `X-Api-Version: 1` would have erased every `1` in every timestamp and attempt count — the floor the credential half of the function already used (8) now applies to the whole value too, with the reason beside it as `minRedactedHeaderValue`, and `TestAShortHeaderValueIsNotRedacted` pins that a tag-length value leaves the gateway's own diagnostics alone. `docs/security.md` §14.1's first bullet says one secret authenticates both directions and there is no second credential, which is still true of what authenticates **to** Brigade but no longer describes what the gateway's machine holds: a bullet beside it (never through it) says what `--header-file` is — a credential Brigade presents and that authenticates nothing to Brigade, under `--secret-file`'s location and mode rules, unable to change what is signed, redacted by the same floor, and sent in the clear under `--allow-http` to a non-loopback target. The operator page's new H3 had been inserted between the `## Run it` flag table and that section's state/resume paragraph, orphaning it; the paragraph is back under its own section, ahead of the H3. The reviewer's two non-blocking notes are left as they are: `post()`'s `Header.Set` per value is reachable only from a hand-built `Config` (`parseHeaderFile` cannot produce a multi-valued key), and `New`'s check is deliberately the name list and not the parser, with `net/http` refusing a malformed name or value at round-trip time. Second review round (Claude, 2026-10-26), one blocker: the flag's usage string carried back-quotes around `Name: value`, and `flag.UnquoteUsage` reads the first back-quoted span as the flag's **value name**, so `--help` printed `-header-file Name: value` — telling the operator the argument is a header line and inviting a third party's credential onto argv, the one place a credential must never be. The usage string now uses double quotes, with the reason beside it, and `TestHelpSaysTheCredentialFlagsTakeAPath` pins that `--help` prints `-header-file string` and `-secret-file string` (nothing covered the gateway's help output before, which is why CI could not catch it). The round's two non-blocking notes are applied as well: `Config.ExtraHeaders`' comment names `parseHeaderFile`, not a non-existent exported `ParseHeaderFile`, and `checkPrivateFile` carries the location reasoning that stood above the one-line `checkSecretFile` wrapper, its parameter renamed from `secretFile` to `file` now that it serves both flags. No release in this commit. Renumbered from P57-1: master already has P57-1 to P57-5 for card 141 | done | Grok Bot | this row's commit |

| P61-11 | **The first real Windows machine, and two bugs it found** (card 133, E12's 2026-10-28 section). A native Windows session of the brigade team (x64, Claude Code in Windows Terminal, plugin 0.41.0 from the marketplace) joined, messaged this session and received its reply: delivery both ways between live sessions. Found there, reproduced and pinned to the lines by that session, verified here: the launcher matched curl's variables by exact case, so under Git Bash's `SYSTEMROOT`/`WINDIR` spelling curl.exe got no system root and resolved no host (the first download failed from the Bash tool and from the SessionStart hook); and `procutil.DetachAttr` combined `DETACHED_PROCESS` with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, which Windows ignores beside it, so the console-less watcher's adapter child opened a visible console window. Fixed: `curlEnvironment` folds case, passes the parent's spelling and supplies `SystemRoot` (neutral test); `environMap` keys upper-cased; `DetachAttr` and the launcher's `detach` are `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | CREATE_NO_WINDOW` (a `procutil_windows_test.go` case); `plugin/bin/brigade.exe` rebuilt by `make launcher`; changelog section `## [0.41.1]`. Released as 0.41.1 by the next commit; the Windows session confirms the window and a cold-cache download after `/brigade:update`. | done | Fable | this row's commit |

| P65-1 | **The state folder, the entry and the facts layer** (card 162, `.context/plans/manager-digest.md` §5.1 and §5.3, row Pn-1; hand-off from session support-duet 2026-10-29). `.brigade.json`'s `sync` member gains `state`, one of its listed folders as listed (`teamfile.SyncConfig.State`; two new unusable tokens, `state_invalid` and `state_not_listed`; carried by `team create --force`). The hook freezes its absolute path into the by-pid map as `state_folder` beside the sync members (a change respawns the watcher). New packages: `internal/harness/state` (the entry type, its caps and sanitiser, the canonical copy under `<state dir>/state/entries/<session id>.json` projected whole-file into `<folder>/sessions/<session id>.json`, the strict folder reader, the two prunes) and `internal/harness/gitfacts` (`Head` reads HEAD and the branch from git's own files through the new `teamfile.GitDirs`, worktrees included, no process; `Runner.Log` is the one `git log`, `since..HEAD` by the checkout's `user.email` as fixed text, newest first, capped at 100, the card parsed from the subject; `AuthorEmail`; `Ignored` for the address rule of P65-5 — an argv array, `adapterkit.ChildEnv`, git found on the hook's own PATH, a nolint on the one `exec.CommandContext` as hook/spawn.go has). The facts layer (`hook/state.go`): SessionStart creates the entry on the register path and refreshes it on the continue path (the end cleared, the name refreshed) then prunes this machine's expired entries; the prompt hook stamps `last_prompt_at`, takes the registry's name, and runs the log only when HEAD moved, last in its budget; SessionEnd records `ended_at` and `end_reason` with the final commits. The watcher reads `state_folder` once and prunes the folder two minutes after its start and hourly (`runState`, two Deps knobs). Verified early, as the hand-off asked: Claude Code's Stop hook input is `stop_hook_active` (snake case; Brigade's camelCase member is Grok's), the block answer is `{"decision":"block","reason":…}` with an 8-continuation cap, and the gateways' harness ids are `gateway-slack`, `gateway-email`, `gateway-webhook`. Deviations from the plan, recorded there: HEAD from git's files rather than `git rev-parse`; commits filtered by author so a pulled teammate commit is not this session's; the canonical copy keyed by session id rather than team and repository. Tests: `TestSyncRules` (the state cases), `TestHeadReadsGitsOwnFiles`, `TestHeadFollowsALinkedWorktree`, `TestLogListsTheRangeByAuthor`, `TestIgnoredAsksGit`, `TestRunnerWithoutGit`, the state package's six, the map's four `state_folder` cases, `TestStateEntryFollowsTheSession`, `TestStateNothingWithoutAStateFolder`, `TestStateFolderCopyIsHealed`, `TestStateContinueRevivesAndPrunes`, `TestStateCommitsSinceStart` (a real repository), `TestStatePrunesTheFolder`. Pages, options and security.md come with P65-6 | done | Fable | this row's commit |
